2026-10-09T13:45:43.4104211Z env:
2026-10-09T13:45:43.4104475Z   UV_PYTHON_INSTALL_DIR: /home/runner/work/_temp/uv-python-dir
2026-10-09T13:45:43.4104816Z   UV_CACHE_DIR: /home/runner/work/_temp/setup-uv-cache
2026-10-09T13:45:43.4112251Z   QWEN_COMMIT_MESSAGE: [require-build] [cluster:aspire2ap] [nodes:2] Verify single-decode attention on SM90 XQA with page size 64 (P3/D1 c72)

Why: the bounded decode Nsight capture (f84ea21, Actions 37931615219/2)
showed that at ~63 running requests the single P3/D1 decode worker spends
7.5 ms per verify step in FA3 FP8-KV attention at only ~0.97 TB/s (~28% of
H100 HBM), and this per-request term sets the ITL slope (~0.155 ms per
running request). MoE is already at the FP8 weight-streaming limit. FA3
keeps its non-TMA paged path for this verify shape (seqlen_q 4 x GQA 16 =
64 <= kBlockM 128) regardless of page size.

FlashInfer 0.6.15's XQA kernel is NVIDIA's GQA decode kernel and supports
SM90, BF16 Q with FP8 KV and multi-token speculative queries, and SGLang's
trtllm_mha backend already routes SM90 target verify to it. At the fixed
source, however, verify calls trtllm_batch_decode_with_kv_cache without the
in-window causal mask, and XQA asserts "Mask is required for speculative
decoding" for q_len_per_req > 1.

Source change (new patch, applied after the four existing ones):
patches/sglang/decode-xqa-verify-mask.patch builds once, after
is_xqa_impl is known, a constant [req_to_token_pool.size, q_len, 2] uint16
bitmask in which row i allows window tokens 0..i, and passes a [:bs] slice
only for non-ragged target verify. The layout equals FlashInfer's own test
generator (tests/attention/test_xqa_batch_decode.py generate_causal_mask);
a CPU check matched it bit for bit for q_len 2..8. Attention still reads
every prefix and window token, so the computation is unchanged apart from
kernel accumulation order.

Runtime change (ASPIRE only, via configs/aspire-serving.json):
- page_size 64 on all workers. trtllm_mha accepts only 16/32/64, and PD
  transfer requires equal page sizes on prefill and decode. Prefill keeps
  FA3 (also non-TMA at page 1), CUTLASS and all prefill kernels.
- Decode --attention-backend trtllm_mha with
  --speculative-draft-attention-backend fa3. In draft extend, trtllm_mha
  casts Q to FP8, which XQA rejects, so the small draft stays on FA3.
- Everything else equals ed8c534: P3/D1, decode pool 393216, Triton
  decode MoE, EAGLE3 steps3/topk1/draft4, FP8 KV, c72, official client.

Expected: if XQA reaches ~2+ TB/s, decode attention drops by ~4 ms/step,
roughly halving the per-request slope (ITL ~17.2 -> ~15.5 ms at 63
running). Judge by score, ITL vs running relation, accept length (a wrong
mask would collapse acceptance) and greedy probes against ed8c534. Page
size and backend change together here; a page-64-only FA3 control is a
follow-up if this candidate helps.

Budget: myprojects 59010014 balance 14183.430 SU, In Doubt 1024 (profile
run not yet settled). This job reserves at most 1024 SU.

Validation: bash -n, Ruff, full CPU pytest including the actual five-patch
chain applied to the fixed source mirror, and the argv fixture with the
new page-size/backend arguments.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
